Russia leads Brazil's October gasoline imports

Itaqui port receives most gasoline volumes

    Brazil is expected to receive at least 125,784 cubic meters of
gasoline imports through Oct. 4, according to preliminary data
from port lineups, sources, and S&P Global Commodities at Sea
released Oct. 2.

    At least three ships are scheduled and confirmed to discharge
imported gasoline at Brazilian ports by October. Most will already
have arrived, berthed, or discharged.

    Russia is set to be the top country of origin, accounting
for 64,151 cubic meters, or 51%, of October imports, and the
Netherlands is expected to be the second-largest importer, with
61,151 cubic meters, or 49%.

                                                                          The G7 has set a four-month deadline for its member states to
                                                                      release the last 100 million barrels of oil stocks they committed
                                                                      to in March after collectively pledging to refrain from energy
                                                                      export restrictions within the organization.

                                                                          In a statement Oct. 2, the seven industrial countries called on
                                                                      the International Energy Agency to "monitor the immediate and
                                                                      full implementation of the March 2026 commitments." Adjusting
                                                                      for stocks already released to the market, the group pledged
                                                                      to oversee the release of 100 million barrels of oil by February
                                                                      next year.

                                                                          The decision included a commitment for the G7 and partner
                                                                      countries to frontload a "substantial diesel release" in the next 20
                                                                      days, with members to discuss the possibility of additional releases
                                                                      in coordination with the IEA. The group will also seek to coordinate
